## Review 1181

Do not book that!! The tour is advertised in the title as being full-day and private. It was not private and not a full day. They picked me up at my hotel at 7:30. And she also booked a private tour. Not acceptable for charging us both so much. I was back at the hotel around 12 o''clock noon... I was picked up by a guide and a driver (as far as normal). When they stopped in the middle of the road, I was irritated because they didn''t tell me anything. So the driver had bought some fruit, we drove on and a third man got in. When I asked him what was going on, he introduced himself as part of the company. That was it. That doesn''t give a tourist traveling alone a good feeling... When we were back in the car after the tour, I just asked what was next and the short answer was back to the hotel. It was not yet 12 noon. I tried to contact the organizers and asked for a refund, but I was ghosted. Booking.com also received no response, which means I am probably left empty-handed. I am very disappointed.

## Review 1195

Did this tour on my last day in Zanzibar and it was truly the highlight of my trip. The Nakupenda sandbank was absolutely stunning, and my guide Abu timed our arrival perfectly, allowing me to capture incredible photos and enjoy a peaceful walk before the crowds arrived. Abu’s great personality made my solo trip feel like an outing with a friend, which I really appreciated. Hotel pick-up and drop-off were smooth, and I learned so much about Zanzibar along the way. It’s an experience that will be hard to beat, and one I fully intend to repeat when I return.

## Review 1196

My family and I had a great time on this tour. The visit to the spice farm was interesting and we enjoyed walking around the beautiful farm to see the variety of spices. The sandbank was the highlight of the tour. The water was calm, shallow and beautiful, and our guide, Abu, took care of everything: drinks, lunch and a shady area to rest after swimming. I highly recommend this tour!

## Review 1197

This was perhaps my favourite tour during my time in Tanzania. It was just me who had booked for that day, so it ended up being a private tour, which meant that the tour and discussion was tailored to my interests. The guide was very informative and took me around all the main points of interest within Stone Town (of which there were many!). He was very knowledgeable on the island''s history and told me he had spent a lot of time in the town''s archives researching, so I was able to learn a lot about Stone Town''s (and Zanzibar''s, more generally) history, which was fascinating. Although, we spoke in depth about the island''s contemporary social life, too. Although several of the buildings were closed for renovation, he incorporated this into the tour and explained why the buildings were specifically being renovated now and what led to their renovation, so I don''t feel I missed anything (although I hope to be back to see them once they''re done). I 100% recommend this tour!

## Review 1263

## Review 1264

This was such a fun experience. The crew was amazing, and very warm, welcoming, and accommodating. The food was the best food we’ve had in Tanzania; my wife, who doesn’t like seafood, thought the lobster and calamari were both incredible.

## Review 1265

AMAZING!!! If you are considering this experience- DO IT! The communication via Viator with Matthew was prompt, our ride to and from was efficient, and the boat experience was amazing. 

John, Crispo, and Sayed (I’m sure I spelled those incorrectly) were professional but so FUN to be with. They certainly know their way around the waters in a safe manner, but make sure you’re having a blast while sailing. We asked them to play their own music, and we all danced the night away learning about their culture.   Best part- THE FOOD. The best food in Zanzibar is on this boat. We had vegetable curry, calamari curry, chicken and kambakochi grilled right on the charcoal fire on the boat. They even surprised us at the end with a cake for our honeymoon. 

AMAZING experience.

-Mike & Casey, Pittsburgh USA

## Review 1266

## Review 1279

La isla es una gran experiencia desde que llegas hasta que se desaparece por el agua.

## Review 1280

First, I’d like to start off by mentioning that even though this says this is a First, I’d like to start off by mentioning that, even though this says this is an English speaking guide, our driver didn’t speak any English! He had someone with him that greeted us that spoke English, but when we got into the vehicle, he didn’t speak any so we couldn’t communicate on our travel plans for the day   
When visiting this port on a cruise ship, there really aren’t many options for tours. The forest tour was just OK, I guess the highlight was seen the red Columbus monkey. We had an energetic tour guide which made it more lively.
The spice farm was very interesting. We had a female guide that took us through all of the trees and vines they grow at this location and learn learned about how the spices are cultivated and used. The only thing I found a little interesting at the end was they didn’t really have a spice shop where you could buy several of the spice trees and shrubs they had on the property.   
Lastly, the stone tour was interesting learning about the slave trade, but our tour guide could’ve been better. He was a little more interested in chatting with his buddies along the tour and didn’t really engage with us as tourists. He was knowledgeable and everything, but it could’ve been a lot more interesting if we had a guy that really got into the history.
  I would probably give this a three star, but I gave it a four star because there really isn’t any other options in this port for tourists.

## Review 1281

What an amazing day, our driver Ahmed met us at the port, excellent communication about where to meet. It was quite rainy and so we made a few adjustments to the order of our day, he was great about calling ahead to verify availability and accessibility as two of us travel with walkers and poc’s. We started at a beautiful spice farm where they had guides to show us around and then we sat under a covered area to learn how the spices were used for cooking and healing. We also tried some amazing fresh fruits. We then drove to Jozani forest (a beautiful ride with so much to see). A guide there showed us around and the rain cleared and we were able to see several monkeys, definitely a highlight for us! We then drove back towards port and Stone Town. On our way out we’d driven through and seen quite a bit but the tour included a guide and short walking tour. Our guide was amazing and started telling us all kinds of stories as we drove through the market area heading to where we had planned to park. The rain decided to return in full force so we opted to return to the ship and be content with what we saw driving through. I can’t believe how much we were able to do given how much it rained and our limited mobility. Ahmed and all of our guides were so kind and helpful, I heartily recommend this tour!

## Review 1282

Sowohl der Fahrer als auch die Guides waren soweit in Ordnung. Die Abholung erfolgte überpünktlich. Allerdings muss ich Abstriche machen. Reserviert hatte ich eine deutschsprachige Führung. Erst kurz vor Abholung erfuhr ich, dass nicht alles auf deutsch erfolgt. Ok. Ich bewundere die Menschen, die unsere Sprache beherrschen. Aber ich muss sagen, dass ich den Guides in Stown-Town nicht gut verstanden habe. Im Jozani-Park war die Führung auf englisch 🤷🏻‍♀️. Auf der Gewürzfarm dann wieder auf deutsch. Und die hat mir wirklich gefallen. Der junge Guide vor Ort sprach auch richtig gut und war kurzweilig. Jetzt leider zu dem, was ich wirklich nicht in Ordnung fand. Es gab weder Wasser im Auto noch wurde mir in Stone-Town die Möglichkeit gegeben, zu Mittag zu essen. Offensichtlich war dies nicht eingeplant. Ok, ich hatte nicht danach gefragt, aber ich dachte auch, dass das dann noch kommt, allerdings waren wir am Ende auf dem Weg zum Fahrzeug und die Gelegenheit war vorbei. So etwas muss man aber auch nicht anfragen, meines Erachtens. Das muss mit eingeplant sein! 
Und wie meine Vorgänger hier schon angemerkt haben; 143€ für eine Person ist schon richtig viel Geld. Dafür sollten die Eintrittsgelder inbegriffen sein. Jedoch musste alles zusätzlich bezahlt werden. Wenn ich den Ablauf gekannt hätte, dann hätte ich mir einfach ein Taxi gebucht. Anbieter gibt es genug. Einfach auch mal im Hotel die Angestellten fragen. Insofern kann ich diesen Anbieter von meiner Seite aus nur bedingt empfehlen.

## Review 1283

This is more of a group activity. If you don’t have a group, you should cancel the class. It’s a lot “gratuities” at every turn. After the spice tour and singing coconut harvest - “This is the portion of the tour where gratuities are greatly appreciated. “ I felt pressured to comply.” The cooking part…chopping up vegetables with dull knife? The girl looked bored and tired as did the vegetables. They were not excited or friendly towards me. The other “cook” talked on her cell phone and tour guide stayed close to ensure that “gratuities were appreciated” at this portion of the tour as well. I don’t do passive-aggressive behavior towards me. I asked for the driver to take me back to my hotel. Explaining to them would have been a waste of energy, breath, and time. I could have used that $60 on a seafood platter. By the way, they were very animated and interactive with white visitors ( not surprised).

Coconut climber, crown weaver, cooking instructors, fruit/Tea guy). We were told by our guide that coconut guy and weaver expected a tip. ## Review 1293

The hosts of this tour (many involved) try very hard to make the tour informative and fun.  The main guide (for us Chiquita) was on top of his game and he was willing to spend as much time as necessary to allow us to learn about the plants rewarding us with high fives when we managed to successfully identify them. He was ably assisted by a young trainee who was one step ahead in collecting the necessary leaves, fruit and roots.  This same young man produced woven baskets, crowns and necklaces for take home treats. 
The cookery lesson was organized by Haitham.  It was not really a cooking lesson but we were allowed to be hands on during the procedure accompanied by the team singing with great energy. Later the food she prepared for lunch was delicious.  We also sang while a young man sprinted up a tree with great strength and ability.  
We were delighted when we realized a group of colobus monkeys were in the surrounding trees. This Union spice farm is  part of a community project and tourism is essential to the livelihood of the local people.
There is an opportunity to buy locally made toiletries and also bags of spice.  Also, we wanted to tip the team members who had made the days so enjoyable.  Before you go make sure you have the means to do this.

## Review 1294

Personnel au top! Petite balade sympa pour voir Stone Town depuis la mer

## Review 1295

I highly recommend this transfer service to The Rock Restaurant. Our driver, Omar, was on time and at our pickup point, waiting for us at the scheduled time. This company waits for you while you enjoy dinner at The Rock Restaurant and will take you back to your destination. We felt safe the entire time.

## Review 1299

Such a fantastic experience! The tour company provides basically a private driver to coordinate with Jozani and the spice farm, but the tours themselves are conducted by a Jozani forest ranger and spice farm guide, respectively. We saw so many monkeys up close and the park ranger was so passionate about conservation of the forest and mangroves. The paths and bridges were well maintained and easy to walk on. The spice farm tour guides were so kind and friendly! There was no real pressure to purchase anything - if you say no they move on. Some haggling is necessary on the spices if you’re interested in purchasing but that’s par for the course in Zanzibar. Would definitely recommend!

## Review 1307

Fantastic down to earth experience with these lovely ladies. Beautifully clean cooking school, with great info and food. Highly recommend to everyone

## Review 1308

Today we were fortunate enough to dive into the local culture! We spent the day with the mamas of Zanzibar. We were dressed in the traditional Hajib, went shopping at the local market, and then spent the day preparing and cooking a meal in their home, using their traditional kitchen appliances! Best experience ever, wonderful ladies! It doesn’t matter where you’re from, what color you are, or what language you speak…. When a bunch of women get together, we are just that a bunch of women! We were laughing and joking, and teaching each other our languages. ❤️ it was an honor to spend the day with these beautiful women. ￼

## Review 1309

I had such a beautiful experience with the Mamas of Zanzibar. 
It’s more than just food – it feels like being welcomed into someone’s home. Attention to details, quality of services, and conversations, a story of empowered women who happen to make dishes that are full of flavor, cooked with so much love, and you can taste the tradition in every bite. What stood out most was the warmth of the women behind it all – kind, genuine, and so passionate about sharing their culture. Thank you, Maskat, Salma, Asyah, and all of the lovely women who hosted us 💗 

If you’re in Zanzibar and want to experience authentic food made with heart, this is where you should go. It’s soulful, delicious, and unforgettable.

## Review 1310

This is so much fun! The mamas are so sweet and hospitable. I can’t imagine another cooking class in Zanzibar even comparing to this one. Fabulous experience. 100% recommend! 

Note: Following the google address on Viator will not take you to the place - so it is best to follow the written directions!!

## Review 1311

I would travel far and wide to experience this tour again. Maskat and the mamas made me feel like family.

## Review 1312

We had a fabulous morning with marvellous mamas, so friendly and welcoming. We shopped at the market for our ingredients then all prepared the most delicious meal. If you’re considering booking, do it! You won’t regret it!

## Review 1313

The Mamas of Zanzibar Cooking Experience was absolutely incredible from start to finish. From the moment we arrived, everyone was so warm and welcoming, making us feel like part of their community. The day started with a walk to the local market, which was such an authentic experience. We picked out the freshest ingredients, learning about the local produce as we went along. 

What made the experience even more special was returning with the mamas to actually prepare the food ourselves. They were so patient and knowledgeable, guiding us step-by-step through traditional Zanzibar recipes. Along the way, they shared their personal stories and the rich history of their culture, adding so much depth to the experience.

The food we made was beyond delicious—flavorful, vibrant, and authentic. I can''t express enough how amazing it felt to be a part of this. I would recommend this 1,000 times over to anyone who loves food, culture, and unique experiences. Truly unforgettable!

Go there, plant a magrove tree! Best tour with Arsheen and Team. Not only a tour, also learn a lot about Mangrove. Verry good equipment.

We enjoyed a tour of a special nature site while experiencing SUP paddling.

## Review 1318

If you want an all inclusive tour that gives you a little bit of everything Zanzibar in single full day adventure, this is your tour and Abdul is your guide. This was an amazing view of so many different parts of Zanzibar. We visited a lovely rural village with beautiful and inviting people living together happily and peacefully. They welcomednua with open arms, shared their food with us and showed us how they sustainably farm to live. The kids played games with us and sang songs that we''re still humming weeks later. We got a spice tour and learned all about the local agricultural products and event went to the markets frequented by locals which gave us a true glimpse into the Zanzibarian lifestyle. We finished the tour in Stone Town and got a chance to talk to more natives, shop for souvenirs and even had a delicious authentic lunch. Our tour guide Abdul was the best we  could have ever hoped for. He spoke English very well and even taught us some words in Swahili. He was knowledgeable, informative and funny as all get out. He was also very flexible and accomodating when it came to seeing additional sights. This was the best tour we had on our vacation! Thanks again Abdul!

## Review 1319

Amazing tour today with our fantastic tour guide Abu (Abubakar) to a community-owned spice farm just outside of Stone Town. We had a brilliant time learning about the history of spice farming in Zanzibar, how certain spices are grown and harvested, got to guess which spice was which by the smell of each plant and seed, and even received a lovely surprise at the end of a handmade gift from our spice farm guide Saïd! We returned to Stone Town, and we were able to talk about what we had already done, and what else we would like to do, and Abu was really helpful in mentioning other places we might like to visit. Abu kept it really exciting and engaging throughout, and we even stopped for some delicious Swahili food for lunch. Thank you Abu! 10/10!

## Review 1320

Erick was an amazing guide. we had a wonderful time. This tour is very safe and super fun for families. The snorkeling was fun with loads of different fish and sea life. The seafood lunch was indeed a feast and so delicious! Erick and his team made us feel safe, gave great insight to the history of Tanzania and provided a great atmosphere of fun. Would highly recommend this one.

## Review 1321

I cannot express how grateful we are for the incredible service we received from our private taxi driver who transformed into our personal tour guide during our entire stay in Zanzibar. From the moment we arrived, he exhibited the highest level of professionalism, friendliness, and an extensive knowledge of Zanzibar’s rich culture and history.

He recommended specific tours that allowed us to explore the breathtaking beauty of the island. Each excursion was thoughtfully curated, showcasing everything from the stunning beaches to the vibrant markets and historical sites. His insights made our visits not only enjoyable but also incredibly enriching, allowing us to connect with the local culture in a meaningful way.

Every day spent with him was filled with laughter, fascinating stories, and unforgettable memories. His passion for Zanzibar was evident, and it truly enhanced our experience.

I wholeheartedly recommend his services to anyone planning a visit to Zanzibar. If you want the best driver around who knows all the hidden gems of the island, look no further! We will definitely be referring him to our friends and family members who plan to visit in the future. Thank you Omar Jumasimba for making our trip AMAZING!

Hopefully they can be released into the wild one day.

## Review 1323

The taxi came on time and welcomed me friendly. Hassan and Yunus (the guide) took me first to the Mnemba Island. We could see some dolphins, but the Waves were kinda high, so I didn''t dare to swim with them.
The next stop was the rock (beach with some nice Landscape), where I took some photos and then we left for the 3rd stop.
This was the Kuzu Cave, an astonishing place with a Long history. YOu can also swim in the water, but it is kinda dark.

The 4th stop was the Yozani Forrest, where we spoted some blue and red Monkeys. We observed them for a while and then headed over.
As the tour was already over and I wasn''t too much interested in other sights, the driver took me to my Ferry in Stone Town.

Yunus and Hassan were both friendly and attentive as well, so I can highly recommend to book with them.

Hint: If you want to have a drop-off at an another place, then just message with the agency, as they could arrange something for you.

## Review 1347

Dhau Tour from Fumbe to Sandbank and Kwale. Downhill at low tide in mud only waist-high water with dinghy possible. Not recommended for physical limitations! Sandbar overflowing, but beautiful snorkel spot with many clownfish and starfish of all colors and sizes. 2. Snorkel spot very currenty. Kwale Island with well organized delicious seafood buffet. Hidden bay unfortunately only briefly navigable at high tide, therefore unfortunately not another snorkel spot. Return journey from Kwale under sail ended very suddenly, as a fastening of the rudder broke out. Hectic hauling in of the sail in quite strong wind and swell.. Exit catastrophically due to the flood again in waist-high water without a jetty very slippery. then we passed garbage dumps and a ruin through bushes to the road where the drivers were waiting for us luckily. Crew very anxious but there is significant need for improvement regarding the departure/return and the condition of the boats.

## Review 1348

Not on western level of quality, way overpriced. Poor quality of amenities, also the food not up to par

## Review 1349

Wunderbares Adventure, toller Ausflug. Es ging kurz nach 8 los. 9:30 Uhr war unser privates Shuttle am Treffpunkt. Dann ging’s los mit einen tollen Segelboot Richtung erste Insel. Auf der Sandbank hatten wir dann 30 Minuten zum  Schauen. Unbedingt Badesachen gleich anziehen , da der Aus- und Einstieg bis zur Brust im Wasser ist. Nun ging’s zur Hauptinsel. Hier wurden wir mit einem kleineren Boot übergesetzt. Es ging zum alten Baum und über die „Shoppingmeile“. Danach gab es leckeres Essen. Hummer, Garnelen, Fisch und Hühnchen. Zwischendrin gab es immer wieder Getränke und frisches Obst. Zum Abschluss ging’s zurück zur Schnorchelstelle. Davor haben uns eine Gruppe Delfine begleitet. Nach ca. 1h Schnorcheln ging’s zurück zum Ausgangspunkt. Die Crew war super und jederzeit ein guter Gastgeber. Diese Tour würden wir wieder machen.

## Review 1367

My tour started slightly after 9:30 once the boats and groups were organized. I was in a group of 9 people, together with an English and Italian speaking guides. Both guides took so much care about us. We had fun on the boat when singing all kind of songs. We visited the prison, has some free time to see around, then we went to see the turtles. After that we went to Nakupenda where we had a nice food prepared directly on the beach. Take suncream with you as there might not be enought space to hide yourself in the shade. Nevertheless, it was nice experience to see the local people to do the barbecue. There was rice, french fries, sauce, seafood and fruits. Water was provided during whole trip. The only thing I expected better was nice snorchelling but unfortunately, there was no place in the sea to do it. One side of the island had big waves and on the other side where the sea was calm, clear and deep enough, there were plenty of boats. Thus do not expect much when it comes to the snorchelling. There were also no starfish during that day. Maybe you will be luckier. Have fun! I can recommend. Once you book, get in touch with the nice tour provider to determine the start of the tour.

## Review 1368

Not great first experience using Viator and its excursions.  
Guide Ali called last night to say I would now be picked up an hour later today, 8 not 7. Left me in a boat this AM with other people for multiple hours as they insisted on seeing dolphins, although I paid a private tour price.  Guide tried to drop me at Jozani forest half hour before my Rock lunch reservation, saying I’d still make it on time after.  Luckily forest tour lady insisted I be taken to my reservation on time and graciously waited and took me for the forest tour after, after being kept waiting all day herself. 
Wasn’t taken to beach or cave in the end. Dropped at hotel at 7pm. Not organized or timely. Not as described. Not worth price. Have filed for a refund and will be contacting all companies involved.

## Review 1369

We were disappointed that there was no email or whats app communication prior to the tour and we went ashore hoping that our confirmed tour would happen. Fortunately it did. We spent nearly an hour going thru traffic just to get away from the port! The driver was very skilled and used whatever routes to get us to the first stop on the tour. The driver was very talented and did many things to try and make it a good tour. The guide was unengaging and the driver really did his best to be informative, humorous, and did his best to share information about the city and the other sites while the tour guide was almost falling asleep. The driver & guide for the dolphin boat was very skilled, friendly and attentive. Our guide did give us a two minute summary of the monkeys we were able to see. 
We would give the driver a 5* review and the guide a 2* review = total 3* for the tour because of the shortcomings of the guide.
We were pleased to be able to see some of the beauty of Zanzibar.

## Review 1370

I decided to take a tour last minute to explore the island. I did the Dolphin Swim, Snorkel, Kuza Cave, Jozani Forest, Paje Beach, Rock Restaurant. My guides were Ibrahim and driver Shafi. They picked me up on time at my hotel, the car was super comfortable. I was by myself because I booked a solo tour. So all attention is to you. I enjoy traveling alone so this was the perfect tour for me. Both guys were kind and helpful. Informed me about the area, answered questions and took pics of me where I wanted. We actually skipped the beach and spent more time at other places. They were flexible with my day. The dolphin trip was incredible. The boat guide was so funny. Knew everyone and helped with snorkeling and found the best spots to swim. For the best snorkeling experience, bring your own glasses. They provided it, but mine kept getting water inside. The cave was super cool. Worth coming back to as they offer music and drum classes and also cooking classes. I was almost alone in the cave. So perfect for you. In the forest we saw all types of monkeys. Ibrahim knew exactly where to find them! Overall one of the best tours. If you want to see a lot of the island in one day, do this tour.

## Review 1371

A wonderful experience with Amina and driver Harfizi. They promptly picked me up and enjoyed getting to know each other on our way to the dolphin tour! With our captain in tow Amina and I set sail to capture a glimpse of dolphins and we were not disappointed. My sea legs are not fully formed and could not handle the various blue seas. Amina, my Dada, took very good care of me as I had to cancel the remaining trip. Having to pullover a few times to rid myself of the last couple of Zanzibar meals they were both supportive and patient with me. I was delivered safe and somewhat sound to my hotel with well wishes! I''m sure we would have had a great day if only I could''ve handled it but all worth it just to see the dolphins and meet the treasures of Zanzibar, Amina and Harfizi!!

## Review 1372

Then we had time to snorkel, had some fresh fruit for lunch, then of to jozani forest to find the monkeys which we did, as we walked through the forest our guide was telling us all about the trees and ferns around us, which was very interesting male and female ferns. then we went onto Kuza Cave which was very nice. You climbed down some steps and then you was in this cave and the water is natural water and its temperature is about 23°. It was lovely and refreshing.

## Review 1375

From start to finish, this tour was absolutely incredible! The day began with an exciting dolphin tour, and seeing these beautiful creatures in their natural habitat was truly magical. We then visited The Rock – such a unique and iconic spot – where the views and atmosphere were simply unmatched.

Paje Beach was a slice of paradise with its white sand and turquoise waters, the perfect place to relax and take in the island’s beauty. Kuza Cave was a hidden gem – the crystal-clear water and peaceful atmosphere made it a must-see. Our journey continued to Jozani Forest, where we had the rare opportunity to see the charming Red Colobus monkeys up close. We then visited The Rock – such a unique and iconic spot – where the views and atmosphere were simply unmatched. where we enjoyed a delicious lunch overlooking the ocean. The highlight was definitely The Rock Special, a fresh and flavorful seafood feast that perfectly captured the taste of Zanzibar.

A special thank you to our guides and drivers, who were professional, friendly, and incredibly knowledgeable. They shared so much about Zanzibar’s history, culture, and wildlife, and made sure every moment was comfortable and enjoyable. Their passion for the island was infectious and really made the trip memorable.

Highly recommend this tour to anyone wanting to experience the very best of Zanzibar in one unforgettable day!

## Review 1376

Thank you for yousef and Ibrahim for the amazing tour! We had a wonderful time watching the dolphins and walking around in the forest and saw the monkeys and had a great time! Ibrahim was amazing and he made the trip unforgettable! The restaurant was amazing too. Thankkk youuuu this trip is a must!

## Review 1377

This tour starts off with bottlenose dolphin spotting which was surreal. Thereafter a snorkeling session near the reef which had plenty of underwater flora & fauna.
Next up was a trek thru Jozani forest and we spotted blue-faced monkeys that are indigenous to the region. I recommend carrying a pair of trek shoes as the terrain is damp and muddy.
Next was Kuza caves which was breathtaking. Recommend wearing footwear with decent grip as the rocks leading to the cave are slippery.
We then made a trip to the Rock restaurant at Pingwe beach where we had our lunch.
The restaurant can be walked to depending on the time of day, later afternoon will reauire a boat ride to access the restaurant [which is free of cost].
Tour ended with a trip to Paje beach.
Highly recommend this tour purely because there’s a variety of activities and terrains to explore.

The dolphin tour was not so good, I saw quite a lot of the dolphins, but they were surround by more than 20 boats, poor them. The captain seems don’t want to speak with us, just kept speaking on the phone or talked to his staff.  Forest, beach and the restaurant were worth to go. Cave was so so….

## Review 1396

## Review 1397

The only good thing is we saw dolphins and monkeys. Bad organization, poor guide, and illegal driving documentation! 
At the beginning we went to the dolphins. We got on boat, no information about what is coming. At some point they started shouting “jump, jump”. We weren’t ready for it, it was very stressful. When we got back to the beach it was heavy raining. We stopped under a shelter for a moment to collect our things. The guide left us there to get to the car, but at the parking there were like 30 same cars so it was hard to find. I was looking for the car for 10 minutes under heavy rain. We got back to the car very wet and the guide stopped to buy himself a tshirt without asking if we need some. So after a horrible cold almost two hour drive we got to the forest. We made very small circles in it and almost no information was given to us. I had to ask a question in order to get soma answer. It was anoying as we were seeing how other guides take care of their groups and how many facts and history lessons they got. 
We knew we will visit the Rock restaurant. We knew lunch wasn’t included, and we have to pay it but we didn’t expect to not have a reservation. 
And the journey back was the real horror. Besides the music in the car everything was very disturbing. As i always follow my GPRS for safety reasons I started noticing that the driver was picking surrounding routes that slowed our return. At some point I realized it was because of the drivers documentation. After a few wrong turns the police caught them anyway. There was a big problem and the driver left the car for a while. After they let us go our guide that was the only one to speak english left the car to go home. After 200 meters the driver suddenly stopped in the middle if the road, turned around and went to some small side road. We we’re hiding there for a while ( i guess from the police) and only when i asked where are we going he said sorry and went back to the road we came from. We felt very unsafe and uncomfortable. 
Last but not least all of the time the guide and the driver were having personal conversations in swahili and only said a few words in English.

Siamo riusciti a vedere i delfini, le stelle marine e le scimmie. Tutto perfetto!

Les petites attentions de notre guide ( fruits locaux) à la recherche des signes dans la forêt de Jozani était comme une partie de cache cache , cuza cave endroit magique une eau limpide et pour finir the rock , une journée magnifique passé avec notre guide francophone Ibrahim je vous conseil vivement il a l’écoute de nos envies.

## Review 1406

Of all the tours we took in Tanzania, this was our least favorite.

Pros:
- Pick up and drop off from our hotel in Nungwi Beach in a roomy vehicle with water provided. The guide and driver were fine. Nothing extraordinary. 
- The dolphin tour included a quick swim in clear, shallow waters with a prepared tray of fruit post-swim. Plenty of opportunity to snorkel, if that’s your thing. 
- We enjoyed our lunch at The Rock. A reservation is recommended. Allow for at least an hour and a half to two hours to leisurely enjoy your meal and take pictures. 
- The cave was beautiful. We didn’t swim, but people around us were enjoying the water. 

Cons:
- Too many activities in one trip. Our tour was 11 hours total from pick up to drop off, of which about half of it was spent driving from one place to another. And this that we skipped out on Paje Beach, at our suggestion. 
- Spent about an hour and a half driving to the dolphin tour from our hotel. The dolphin tour was a bit sad. We spotted one dolphin and all boats raced to corner it. It seemed very intrusive. Also, our captains didn’t seem interested in interacting with us, compared to what we saw going on in other boats. 
- We spent about 45 mins driving to the Jozani Forest from the dolphin tour, whilst spending about 10 mins there observing the red and blue monkeys. Another 10 mins were spent walking through the mangroves. It took us another 45 mins to drive to The Rock for lunch. 
- It took a little over two hours to drive back to our hotel.

## Review 1407

The itinerary was great and our guide was pleasant. Would have been a 4-5 if not for the safety concerns. We were put on a small motor boat with no life jackets and driven out into the middle of the ocean amidst big waves to look for dolphins. My wife doesn’t swim and the waves were splashing inside the boat. We asked to go back to shore, and even though we were the only two passengers, the driver insisted on continuing to look for dolphins. Viator should not promote unsafe activities. This would have been fine if we were given life jackets. Some other tours had life jackets and/ or larger boats.

Jiga boy & Salomon made my trip perfect.

## Review 1414

Très bonne excursion avec Ibrahim, qui arrive à l''heure à l''hôtel, qui est gentil et à l''écoute des envies de ses clients. Un vrai bonheur de voir les dauphins, les poissons et les singes. Visite de la mangrove également. Pensez à prendre des chaussons aquatiques pour se baigner dans les grottes. La réservation à The Rock n''est pas comprise. Bonne excursion !

## Review 1415

INCROYABLE! Le programme semble chargé mais honnêtement, cela passe très vite sans avoir l''impression de courir toute la journée. Ils sont venua nous chercher à l''hôtel en voiture puis on s''est sirigé vees Mnemba où on a pris un bateau. On a nagé avec les dauphins plusieurs fois, puis on est allé nous détendre dans un coin tranquille avec une eau cristalline de 2m de peofondeur. Ensuite snorkeling. Nous sommes ensuire allés voir les singes dans la forêt de Jozani puis à Paje, voir le restaurant The Rock et comme nous n''étions pas spécialement intéressé d''y ma ger à cause des prix attrape-touristes, notre guide nous a proposé un autre resraurant en bord de plage avec des pris bien plus abordables. On a fini par la grotte de Kuza pour une baignade rafraîchissante. Notre guide Charisma parlait parfaîtement français et était particulièrement sympathique et souriant! Nous avons décidé de reprogrammer une activité avec lui. Il nous a donné beaucoup d''informations sur le pays, sa culture, ses activités etc. Nous avons fait les déplacements entre les divers lieux à visiter en voiture, ce qui nous a permis de voir du pays et de constater comment les gens vivent. Je recommande vivement!

## Review 1416

## Review 1423

## Review 1424

We were only spending a few days in Zanzibar so we decided to do this excursion after reading the positive feedback.

The chofer picked us up at 8:00 and we went straight to catch a boat and see the dolphins. Unfortunately, the sea was rough so we couldn’t see any, we did some snorkeling instead. After that we went to Jozani forrest, saw the different types of monkeys. The monkeys were great, we spent 30 minutes there only but we didn’t feel we needed to spend more time. 
Since we had made a booking at the Rock Restaurant at 2:00pm, we had time to go to Kuza Cave before lunch. We enjoyed it! Very few people and the cave was very nice to swim in. 
We had lunch at the Rock which was good, you have to know you pay for the views but the food was also good. At about 3:00pm we went to our last stop, Paje beach, and stayed there for 45 minutes. It’s a normal beach and wasn’t bad, but something you can skip if you don’t have time or don’t feel like going. There were also quite a lot of people trying to sell you stuff. We then went to our hotel at Nungwi and arrive at 5:45pm.

They day in general was good, it was a pity we didn’t see the dolphins but the rest of the activities were great (except for Paje which was OK). We felt the guide could have explained us a bit more about the cave or Jozani forrest, but he was ok. Also, we thought it’s expensive taking into account it does not include lunch (remember to book the Rock restaurant previously). But we enjoyed the day and recommend the experience.

## Review 1425

Een jonge dame was de gids, ze zei bijna niks, was eigenlijk het verlengstuk van de chauffeur. Ik had gereserveerd in het Rock restaurant en houd er rekening mee dat je blijkbaar ook de gids trakteert rond de 60 dollar extra kosten. Dolfijnen trip een echte afrader, 20 boten met gemiddeld 7 mensen die 1-3 dolfijnen opjagen. Zonde van de dag, kost je ongeveer 3 uur vanaf je hotel, terwijl de dag al best lang is, rond 19.00. De gids was verlegen en gaf geen info, alleen als je het vraagt.

## Review 1427

It was a very impressive tour, which our driver together with our guide Ibrahim made unforgettable, thanks a lot for that.
The dolphin-tour is a bit questionable as it appeared more like a hunt than like a watching tour. Something the tour agency might want to think about, we had some respectful animal watching tours in other regions of the world which we liked much more. The buffet arranged by our guide on the boat with fresh fruit was delicious. The other attractions where also really nice and enjoyable, visiting monkeys and mangrove regions in Jozani, followed by the Rock, where you should make a reservation when willing to eat there and tell the guide at which time you want to be there for how long. Next we went to Kuza Cave, which is also an impressive site, and at the end we visited Paje Beach, also a very nice place where locals and tourists enjoy the beauty of an endless beach. Ibrahim told us a lot about these places and about life on Sansibar in general, as he was also very interested in information we shared with him. This trip was exciting and fun, thanks again to our diver and our guide.
Just one more very positive aspect, the tour started exactly at iam as planned and during the entire trip Ibrahim was always present and available, he took perfect care of us!

## Review 1433

I recommend this tour because of my amazing guide Ibrahim! I was contacted the day I booked suggesting I make a reservation for the Rock if I actually wanted to eat there. Even with the time constraint because of the reservation I was able to do everything I wanted to do and the guide and driver listened to what my priorities were. I never once felt rushed. The dolphin experience is with a guide on the beach and your main guide will stay behind. I opted not to swim with the dolphins because it didn’t feel safe for humans or dolphins. If you choose not to swim with the dolphins then I would suggest not wearing your swimsuit. If you want to swim in the cave they have changing rooms for you! The monkey experience was amazing! The cave was really cool but quite the steep walk so bring good walking shoes (these will be helpful in the forest as well). I opted to eat at the Rock and loved it! There are accesible bathrooms at every stop, which is important because there is a lot of driving on this tour! Overall I recommend this tour!

## Review 1441

## Review 1442

We did a half-day private dolphin watching and snorkeling tour in Zanzibar, and overall it was a mixed experience.
The guide was friendly and helpful, although he did disappear for quite a while at one point, which left us a bit unsure about what was happening. The snorkeling itself was definitely a highlight, seeing the colourful fish and marine life was absolutely beautiful and made that part of the trip very worthwhile.
We understand that dolphins are wild animals and sightings can’t be guaranteed, but we didn’t see any during our tour and weren’t informed until we were already heading back to shore. It would be helpful if this was communicated more clearly upfront so expectations are managed.
On a positive note, the guide extended our tour and offered us the chance to feed sea turtles (for an additional cost), which turned out to be a really great and memorable experience.
Overall, a good trip for snorkeling, with a few areas where communication and organization could be improved.

## Review 1443

From start to finish, this excursion was truly a once-in-a-lifetime experience. My tour guide was on time, thorough, and incredibly fun — the kind of guide who instantly makes you feel comfortable and excited for the day ahead. On the ride to the beach, he introduced me to Zanzibar’s music and local artists, turning the drive into an interactive cultural moment. We even stopped at a few local shops beforehand so I could experience real life outside the resort, talk with locals, and grab my fins. He also picked up fresh fruit from a market — I didn’t know what it was for at the time, but it turned out to be such a sweet surprise later.

When we arrived at the beach, we met the rest of our small group — a lovely Portuguese couple — and boarded our private boat, which had that perfect, authentic Zanzibar vibe. The water was a bit darker that day, but still so unbelievably clear and blue that it felt unreal. And let me tell you: our guide was NOT afraid to get in the mix for the best photos. I swear he jumped into the water at least 12 times just to capture the perfect shots.

Swimming with dolphins in the wild was something I’ll never forget. I’ve never been that close to them before, and seeing them in their natural environment was mesmerizing and almost surreal.

After our dolphin adventure, we headed to Namba Island, where we snorkeled through the most beautiful coral and schools of fish. The water, the colors, the calm — it was one of those rare moments where you’re completely present and grateful just to exist in the moment.

The entire day was amazing. If I could’ve done this excursion more than once during my vacation, I absolutely would have. And next time I’m in Zanzibar, this will be one of the very first things I book again.

Highly, highly recommend — this experience is the definition of unforgettable.

When we reached shore we discovered that our tour was to be a walking tour of 3 hours and there was no air conditioned vehicle. At 31 degrees we would never have booked this tour to walk especially with the high humidity. 
While we were walking he ran into a store and bought us a 1.5 litre of water. Rather large to carry but there was no place to sit down and have a drink. Also, there was no washroom break - we are saying that the opportunity was not presented to us as it was to be a 3 hour walk.

After spending 30 minutes with the turtles, they took us to the most beautiful beach, Mtende Beach, definitely well worth a visit.

## Review 1501

We spent two nights in Stone Town after a beach stay on the East Coast and had read that a walking tour was a must-do to appreciate the history and culture of the area. After reading glowing reviews, we decided to book with Nouman and were looking forward to the experience.
Unfortunately, things got off to a rough start. We were told to meet at 9:00 AM and arrived promptly, but Nouman showed up 10–15 minutes late. While he mentioned later that he had bike trouble, we had sent texts to check in and received no response — a simple message would have gone a long way in setting expectations and starting things off on a better note.
Throughout the tour, Nouman frequently took phone calls, which made us feel like we weren''t a priority. He would share a brief bit of information and then return to his phone, making it difficult to stay engaged or feel like we were having a personalized experience. Admittedly, we may not have been the most enthusiastic group, but it’s hard to be when you feel like an afterthought.
At $50 USD, we expected more value — both in terms of professionalism and depth of information. In hindsight, exploring on our own with a guidebook or online resources might have been more enjoyable and cost-effective.

## Review 1523

This two-hour walking tour is a great way to be introduced to Zanzibar. I was fortunate to have Ferangi (sp) as my tour guide. He provided a historical overview of Zanzibar, explained the architecture, including the beautiful Zanzibar style doors, and took me thorough a fun and interesting tour of the maze of Zanzibar streets and local neighborhood sites. The tour included visiting the East Africa slave trade museum which Ferangi gave me the option on how much time I needed to review the exhibits in museum, and in the church, he provided more information about this sad and dark part of human history. We went to the Souk (open air market) where Ferangi helped me find some tea, visited the beautiful park by the ocean, and visited the Fort, all while hearing about Zanzibar''s history and other local information.  I highly recommend this tour, and apologies if I misspelled my guides'' name.

Our driver was excellent. Our spice farm tour was fantastic and the guide was excellent. A must-do!!

## Review 1534

This was such a beautiful and relaxing experience, we did not want it to end.  The staff was so kind and made us feel like we were the captains of the ship.  We had 4 adults and 4 teen/tweens and everyone loved it.  The ship was so comfortable and the scenery was a dream.  There was enough space for everyone to have a spot to lounge and even lie down.  The fruit and snack provided we fresh and perfect for the trips.  Everyone fought over the cookies and we were thankful they kept some packaging so we were able to check for allergies for my son who has nut allergies.  The water was clear and beautiful, but watch out.  If you feel stinging/a bite like pinch turns out its the larvae from stingy (urchin/jellyfish).  The crew tried to tell us but we didn''t understand.  Ask them to stop somewhere that doesn''t have sea urchins.  You can actually see them at the bottom.  

The crew had supplies for snorkling and even a floatie.  It was one of the best experiences and I definitely recommend this cruise.

## Review 1540

Day tour to three of the highlights in Zanzibar. Everything went perfectly. Our guide Hasan conveyed so much valuable information in very good German. The first destination was a spice farm. We were explained about fruits, spices, cultivation methods, and so much more.
The second destination was Prison Island. After a short boat tour, we experienced the island''s history up close, and the giant tortoises are simply enormous; some are over 150 years old. Tip: Lunch directly on the dock at the restaurant. Stonetown is hard to put into words, with such stark contrasts, and in every corner, you can feel the tumultuous history.
Summary: everything was great! 
We were picked up on time, taken care of all day without stress, and the price/performance ratio was also okay, Top-notch!

Because of this, we just didn''t have that extra time to see Mtende Beach. We had to choose the first two places that we definitely want to see. Then, we had to opt for a place closer to our port terminal for our last stop, a 5-minute photo stop at the Stone House. No apologies from this tour guide and no partial refund (requested) was given to us so far. I do not recommend going with this tour operator. Their lack of communication can really set you back.

## Review 1557

Led by an experienced guide, with a separate driver for ease in stone town, we travelled from my hotel in the far north east to the three stops. 
The spice farm was rustic, with a walk through the grounds picking out the plants growing for their aromatics.  
Stone town, in its chaos, is beautiful, but many of the attractions are undergoing renovation. However, walking through the city gives a great flavour of the islands culture and history. 
Prison island, other than being a small zoo for a few animals, doesn’t very well showcase its own history, but the guide explained the complex changing of hands the island has experienced. 

It’s rare I’d commend a driver in Zanzibar, but we had a safe (and courteous) driver who kept us safe when the skies opened up. 

Fantastic day.

## Review 1558

The driver and guide were great. They adapted superbly to our wishes. Spice Farm was the highlight. At the end we even had fresh pineapple.

Loved the spice tour! So interesting and interactive. Enjoyed Prison Island and learning about its history as well. The boat ride was a little rough but otherwise, had a great time and learning experience with Khamis.

## Review 1561

The guide was very knowledgeable, talkative and nice. We stayed in Stone Town for 2 nights to visit the things near by, and this tour was perfect for that. That said, it is quite a long tour (from 9am to 3pm) so it would have been nice if the guide indicated us a traditional place (and safe food wise) to eat or else we advise for the people to bring some snacks with them.

## Review 1562

We had an excellent day exploring Zanzibar with Guides via Viator, our wonderful guide Khamis, and Jonas the driver. Everything was perfectly organized, and they made the whole experience smooth, fun, and informative.

The Spice Farm was such a highlight – we saw and learned about so many different spices, tasted and smelled them fresh, and really appreciated the depth of knowledge our guide shared.

At Prison Island, the giant tortoises were simply unbelievable – such gentle giants, and being able to get up close with them was a once-in-a-lifetime experience.

Finally, wandering through Stone Town was magical. The culture, history, and vibrant colours brought the whole city to life – every corner had something new to see, from bustling markets to beautiful architecture.

Overall, this tour gave us the perfect balance of nature, culture, and history. A huge thank you to Khamis and Jonas for making it such a memorable day – highly recommend!

I'd recommend this trip.

## Review 1572

Enjoyed the walk through Stone Town and the spice farm. Our guide and driver were both excellent. Sadly we can’t rate this tour higher than 4 stars as the boat ride to and from Prison Island was horrible. ## Review 1573

This was a FANTASTIC day ! We were picked up from our hotel on time at 8:30am by our guide Yaya and our lovely driver Yumah (?).
Yaya was simply a brilliant tour guide. He ‘self taught’ several languages from YouTube (eg. English, French and Hungarian) and he spoke better English than we did .. with the occasional ‘cockney’ accent … we had such a laugh all day long. First stop after a 30 minute drive was Maganga Spice Farm. Our host took us around the farm sampling various spices and Yaya told us all about them. And to make it more fun, he tested us to see if we could guess what they were. Obviously I got more right than my wife 😉, but Yaya said it was a draw ! 🙄. On our way around we saw some beautiful young children playing on the farm .. they were so inquisitive of us and seemed so happy 😍 .. We were very sad that we had nothing to give them as we’re weren’t expecting to see them, so if anyone takes this tour, please take some sweets/candy with you, the children will very much appreciate it. All in all we sampled Lipstick plant, Turmeric, Lychee, Cardamon, Vanilla (creeper plant), Cotton Tree, Cinnamon .. bark, root, leaves (Queen of the Spice), Black pepper (creeper plant), Coffee, Langilangi flower (for perfume), Clove tree (King of the Spice), Nutmeg & Ginger - Yaya educating us on the plant, harvest and uses of the spices. It was fascinating. At the end of this leg of the tour, one of the farmers climbed up a tree (singing on the way !) and picked a coconut for us the drink the milk from. Whilst our other farmer guide, made headware and ‘jewellery’ for us from leaves. We visited the spice shop on the way out. A great start to the day ! 
Next stop the port of Stone Town to get a boat to Prison Island. This was great fun too, even though my wife wasn’t keen on the 15-20 minute speed boat crossing. Whilst there, Yaya sorted out all the entrance tickets/fees and then told us all the amazing history of Prison Island .. I would never have thought that despite the name, no convicted criminals were ever ‘housed’ on Prison Island .. it was solely slaves and used as a place of quarantine when Yellow fever and Cholera broke out. After the ‘prison’ walk we had around 30 minutes to enjoy the amazing tortoises on the island .. they were enormous.. and the oldest one is around 200 years old. After the short trip back to the main island, we enjoyed the 3rd and final leg of the tour around Stone Town. Yaya kindly helped us negotiate a price on 2 football shirts. A lesson for us Brits here, as we are not used to bargaining on a price .. but in Zanzibar it’s a way of life ! We stopped at a very old building that Yaya called the ‘wonder’ building .. the building is under significant renovation at the moment .. and if I remember correctly, it was the place of a very very short war ! (Sorry if I got that wrong Yaya, as you shared sooo many amazing facts, that it’s sometimes difficult for old people like us, to remember everything 🙏). From there we visited an area that was previously a ladies tennis court (of all things !), and then through the narrow streets of Stone Town, passing Freddie Mercurys birthday place/museum, onto the market area (vegetable, meat and fish) before finishing at the humbling Slave Trade Market .. where an amazing Christian Church sits alongside a mosque and both religions respect each other and happily co-exist. A lesson for the rest of the world ! 
Our drive back to Kiwenga took around 1-1.5 hours, getting out of the hustle and chaos of Stone Town.. through quieter/remote farm towns and villages. We saw so many things that humbled us and made us value and appreciate the simple things in life, a lot more than we did previously. 
Yaya didn’t stop talking or sharing information the whole day long, as well as making us laugh with his ‘international’ impressions. He even helped us out to pay for a couple of small items when we only had $20 bills (we obviously paid him back !). He was ‘Simply The Best’ and we are so grateful to Yaya for making it a special day. Thank You.. Asante ! 🙏
